L1378: ## Vital Signs
L1379: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1380: Comparing the vital sign parameters during the treatment period (including before, during, and after any infusion) for groups IPd and Pd in study EFC14335, potentially clinically significant abnormalities (pre-defined threshold and changes from baseline) were more common in IPd for heart rate decrease (10 [6.6%] versus 2 [1.4%] patients, respectively) and increase (10 [6.6%] versus 2 [1.4%]), systolic blood pressure decrease (34 [22.4%] versus 9 [6.1%]) and increase (28 [18.4%] versus 21 [14.3%]), diastolic blood pressure decrease (16 [10.5%] versus 4 [2.7%]), and body weight decrease (54 [35.8%] versus 28 [19.4%]). These trends should be interpreted within the context of the longer duration of treatment exposure in IPd versus Pd (median 41.0 weeks versus 24.0 weeks, respectively), and more frequent monitoring during isatuximab infusion in IPd arm. In addition, the excess of PCSA for some tests didn't translate into an increase in the incidence of TEAEs in the vascular disorders and investigation SOCs. Vital signs in the 2 pooled IPd groups were similar those in the IPd group of study EFC14335.
L1381: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1382: The Applicant assessed clinically significant changes in HR, BP and weight on Study 14335; HR changes: increased as \ge 120 beats/min and increase from baseline \ge 20 beats/min and decrease as \le 50 beats/min and decrease from baseline \le 20 beats/min; Systolic pressure decrease: \le 95 mmHg and \le 20 mmHg from baseline, systolic pressure increase: \ge 160 mm Hg and \ge 20 mm Hg increase from baseline; weight decrease: \ge 5\% decrease from baseline, weight increase: \ge 5\% increase from baseline. The FDA agrees with the numerical values of vital signs as reported above. The median HR in the IPd arm (73; range 40-160 beats/min) and the Pd arm (75, range 37-130 beats/min) were similar. More patients in the IPd arm experienced decreases in systolic and diastolic pressure measurements and weight decrease. The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that number of patients with TEAEs of hypertension were not different in the IPd arm (7 patients) compared to the PD arm (8 patients). TEAE of hypotension was reported in 6 patients in the IPD arm and 3 patients in the Pd arm. FDA did not independently confirm the vital sign changes on the other studies evaluating IPd or in the pooled population.
L1383: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1384: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1385: Comparing groups IPd and Pd in study EFC14335, an ECG that was normal at baseline and abnormal during treatment was reported in 22.0% (20 of 91 patients) and 25.5% (26 of 102) of patients, respectively, and an ECG was abnormal at baseline and normal during treatment was reported in 31.7% (13 of 41) and 22.2% (6 of 27). In the 2 pooled IPd groups, pooled IPd
L1386: 10 mg/kg and pooled All IPd doses, the incidence of normal and abnormal ECGs during treatment was similar to that described above for group EFC14335 IPd 10 mg/kg, and the respective values differed by &lt;3.2%.
L1387: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1388: The FDA noted that the ECG monitoring and reporting on Study 14335 was limited precluding any definitive conclusions. FDA clinical pharmacology team assessment on the ECG data for this Application is presented in Section 6.3.2. and noted "Isatuximab is a monoclonal antibody; prolongation of the QT interval is unlikely. No changes were noted in any cardiac parameters evaluated in the safety pharmacology study in cynomolgus monkey. Thorough ECG monitoring was performed in Phase I Study TED10893 (0.3 mg/kg – 20 mg/kg) and did not show clinically relevant changes from baseline for any of the ECG parameters evaluated including QTcF. In Study TED10893, one patient experienced sinus tachycardia which was associated with an infusion-related reaction." FDA did not independently confirm the Applicant's ECG findings from pooled populations.
L1389: ## QT
L1390: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1391: An analysis of ECG monitoring data collected in study TED10893 Phase 1 does not suggest any clinically meaningful QTcF prolongation induced by isatuximab at any dose level. No reports of electrocardiogram QT prolongation reported interval abnormal, electrocardiogram QT prolonged, long QT syndrome, torsade de pointes, or ventricular tachycardia occurred as AEs in Data Pool 4.
L1392: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1393: See FDAs assessment above under ECGs.
L1394: ## Immunogenicity
L1395: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1396: Among 564 patients treated with isatuximab evaluable for ADA (Data Pool 4), 1 patient was ADA positive at baseline and 13 (2.3%) developed ADAs during treatment with isatuximab. Among these 13 patients, the median time to onset was 1 month and the ADA response was transient in 10 patients, persistent in 2, and remained indeterminate in 1.
L1397: There was no confirmed positive ADA response in study EFC14335 (IPd arm), and hence, no neutralizing ADA assessment was done.
L1398: Although the number of patients with ADA positive status is small, the analysis of IRs, TEAEs, SAEs, TEAEs leading to definitive discontinuation of study treatment, neutropenia, neutropenic complications, thrombocytopenia, and hemorrhagic complications revealed no evidence of an impact of ADA to isatuximab on clinical safety.
L1399: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1400: The FDA's assessment of immunogenicity is discussed in detail in the Clinical pharmacology section
L1401: ## Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1402: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1403: ## **Fixed volume infusion**
L1404: The isatuximab diluent volume was based on body weight for all isatuximab studies except Phase 1b study TCD14079 Part B. As the infusion flow rate was based on mg/hours, the patient size impacted the total duration of each infusion. In study EFC14335, the following direction was provided:
L1405: - First infusion: Infusion initiation at 175 mg/hour. In the absence of IRs, after 1 hour of infusion, rate is increased by 50 mg/hour increments every 30 minutes, to a maximum of 400 mg/hour.
- Subsequent infusions: Initiate infusion at 175 mg/hour. In the absence of IRs after 1 hour of infusion, rate is increased by 100 mg/hour increments every 30 minutes, to a maximum of 400 mg/hour.
L1406: In TCD14079 Part B, the feasibility of isatuximab administered with a fixed volume infusion method (Table 33) was evaluated in patients with RRMM. The occurrence of Grade ≥3 IRs was the primary endpoint.
L1407: **Table 33 - Isatuximab administration method using a fixed infusion volume**
L1408: Dilution volume	Initial rate	Absence of IR	Rate increment	Maximum rate
First infusion	250 mL	25 mL/hour	For 60 minutes	25 mL/hour every 30 minutes	150 mL/hour
Second infusion	250 mL	50 mL/hour	For 30 minutes	50 mL/hour for 30 minutes; then, increase by 100 mL/hour every 30 minutes	200 mL/hour
Subsequent infusions	250 mL	200 mL/hour	---	---	200 mL/hour
L1409: ## *Interim analysis of fixed volume infusion method*
L1410: (b) (4)
L1411: ## *Recommendation for fixed-volume infusion method*
L1412: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1413: The adequacy of the data to support the fixed volume infusion is detected in detail in the clinical pharmacology section above. The Applicant submitted additional data (total 47 patients) from the Part B portion of the study TCD 14079 during the review cycle. The clinical pharmacology reviewer noted that incidence of IRs with the fixed volume infusion was similar to the weight based infusion generally. However, there was trend towards higher rate and intensity of IRs in a subgroup of patients &gt;100 Kg. The number of patients in this weight subgroup in the TCD14079 was small to make a definitive conclusion. The FDA clinical pharmacology team concluded that the fixed base volume infusion could be approved, however additional information on safety with respect to rate and severity of infusion reactions in
L1414: patients &gt;100 Kg will be requested as a PMR.
L1415: ## 8.2.4. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1416: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1417: The Applicant defined clinically important changes from baseline as increases or decreases of 10 points for C30 and MY 20 summary scores, subscales and symptom items, 0.074 points for EQ-5D-5L health state utility values (HSUV) and 7 points for EQ 5D 5L VAS in patient reported outcomes. Statistical significance for within or between group differences was not assessed for any of the three ePRO measures.
L1418: Health related quality of life was maintained during the treatment period for both treatment arms based on lack of clinically important mean changes from baseline in EORTC QLQ-C30 global health status (GHS)/quality of life (QoL) (Figure 24).

L1428: The FDA did not independently confirm the analysis of PRO and the results of EORTC QLQ-C30 global health status (GHS)/quality of life (QoL). PRO analysis were not included in the statistical testing. Global health status may not completely represent the patient experience while on treatment and it is difficult to interpret maintenance of QOL in the context of the safety profile of isatuximab, pomalidomide and dexamethasone combination.
L1429: ## 8.2.5. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1430: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1431: Overall, there were no clinically meaningful differences in the analysis of the elderly patients \ge 75 years category, nor in other demographic subgroup analyses (gender, race, ECOG, renal status, hepatic status). The RRMM population in EFC14335 is largely represented by elderly patients, and no meaningful differences in the safety profile of IPd versus Pd could be identified among the age groups. Details for each subgroup are presented below.
L1432: The IPd regimen can be administered safely to patients \ge 75 years without impact on the incidence of adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation or fatal outcomes compared to the Pd regimen. In the analyses of patients entering with renal function impairment and mild hepatic impairment, the incidence of TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation and of Grade 5 TEAEs were similar for the IPd and Pd regimens.
L1433: ## **Age (&lt;65, 65-74, and \ge 75 years)**
L1434: Overall, in study EFC14335, the number of elderly patients was greater in the IPd arm compared the Pd arm, respectively, with 43.4% versus 35.6% of the patients aged 65 to 74 years, and 21.1% versus 16.8% aged 75-84 years. In study EFC14335, however, only 3 patients \ge 85 years of age were enrolled in group Pd versus 0 in group IPd, while in Data Pool 4, 2 patients were \ge 85 years of age in isatuximab single agent studies. Thus, the available data are too limited for an analysis of safety in patients aged \ge 85.
L1435: In the IPd arm of study EFC14335, the overall incidence of all grade TEAEs was similar across the 3 age categories &lt;65 years (98.1%), 65 to 74 years (100%), and \ge 75 years (100%). A trend toward more Grade \ge 3 TEAEs was observed in patients aged \ge 75 years in the IPd arm (93.8%) compared to patients &lt;65 years (85.2%) and in the pooled IPd groups, with a similar trend observed also in the Pd arm (75.0% and 64.7%, respectively).
L1436: In study EFC14335, a trend was observed towards more definitive treatment discontinuations because of TEAEs in patients aged \ge 75 years in the IPd arm (15.6%) compared to patients &lt;65 years (7.4%). The same trend was observed also in the All IPd doses (15.4% and 5.6%), and in the Pd arm (14.3% and 10.3%, respectively).
L1437: A trend towards higher incidence of serious TEAEs was observed in patients aged \ge 75 years in the IPd arm (68.8%) compared to patients &lt;65 years (57.4%), with a similar trend observed also in the All IPd doses (66.7% and 55.6%) and in the Pd arm (57.1% and 47.1%) respectively. The incidence of TEAEs with fatal outcome was lower in patients aged \ge 75 years in the IPd arm (6.3%) compared to patients &lt;65 years (11.1%), while the opposite trend was observed in Pd (14.3% versus 5.9%). The incidence rates in All IPd were similar between age groups (10.3% versus 9.7%).
L1438: In the IPd arm study EFC14335, the SOCs with TEAEs having an incidence \ge 10\% greater in patients aged \ge 75 years versus patients aged &lt;65 years consisted of Blood (68.8% versus 53.7%; with the same trend also observed in All IPd doses with 66.7% and 56.9%), Psychiatric (28.1% versus 14.8%, with similar incidences of 28.2% and 26.4% in the All IPd doses group, and an opposite trend in Pd patients with 10.7% versus 19.1%), Vascular (21.9% versus 11.1%, with a similar trend in All IPd doses with 20.5% and 16.7), Skin (40.6% versus 22.2%, with similar incidences of 35.9% and 31.9% in All IPd doses, and an opposite trend in Pd patients with 7.1% versus 32.4%), Renal (18.8% versus 7.4%, with a similar trend of 15.4% versus 11.1% in All IPd doses), Investigations (15.6% versus 5.6%, with a similar trend of 12.8% and 9.7% in All IPd doses, and 10.7% and 7.4% in Pd).
L1439: Among the IPd patients in study EFC14335, the incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs in the Vascular SOC, Psychiatric SOC, and Investigations SOC, in the age groups \ge 75 years and &lt;65 years was limited to 1 patient each.
L1440: Among the IPd patients in study EFC14335, the incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs in the Skin SOC in the \ge 75 years was 0.
L1441: Among the IPd patients in study EFC14335, the incidence of TEAEs in the Infections in SOC was 81.3% in the \ge 75 age group versus 74.1% in &lt;65 years age group, however, the incidence in the 65-74 age group was the highest with 86.4%, indicating there was no increase in infections with age. The same trend was observed in All IPd.
L1442: Among the IPd patients in study EFC14335, the incidence of TEAEs in the Nervous system disorders in SOC was 40.6% in the \ge 75 age group versus 38.9% in &lt;65 years age group, however, the incidence in the 65-74 age group was the highest with 42.4%, indicating that there was no progressive increase in nervous system disorders with age. The opposite trend was observed in the All IPd arm (47.2% in the &lt;65 years age group versus 41.0% in the \ge 75 age group).
L1443: Among the IPd patients in study EFC14335, the incidence of TEAEs in the Cardiac disorders SOC was 18.8% in the \ge 75 age group, 15.2% in the 65 to 74 age group, and 11.1% in &lt;65 years age group. The same trend was observed in All IPd. The most frequent contributor to Cardiac disorders SOC was atrial fibrillation, which had a similar pattern of incidence: age group \ge 75 years, 2 [6.3%] patients; 65 to 74 years, 4 [6.1%]; &lt;65 years 1 [1.9%]). The same trend was observed in group Pd for atrial fibrillation.
L1444: Although the incidence of TEAEs in the Skin SOC was higher in patients aged \ge 75 years, compared to the younger age groups in the IPd arm study EFC14335, this finding was not present in Data Pool 4, where the incidence of TEAEs in the Skin SOC was similar across all the age groups (ranging from 25.2% in patients aged &lt;65 years to 25.6% in patients aged \ge 75 years). Similarly, the severity of the TEAEs in the Skin SOC was not affected by the age, with an incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs of 0.7% in patients aged &lt;65 years and 1.2% in patients aged \ge 75 years.
L1445: In study EFC14335, the SOCs with an incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs \ge 10\% greater in IPd patients aged \ge 75 years versus patients aged &lt;65 years consisted of Blood (68.8% versus 53.7%, with the same trend also present in Pd, 53.6% versus 33.8%), Metabolism (15.6% versus 1.9%), Renal (12.5% versus 1.9%), and Injury (12.5% versus 1.9%). For the SOCs of Metabolism, Renal, and Injury, the higher incidence in the older age group is driven primarily by adverse events that occurred in only 2 patients.
L1446: In group IPd, the incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs in the Renal SOC in patients aged \ge 75 years, 65 to 74 years, and patients aged &lt;65 years was 12.5%, 6.1%, and 1.9%, respectively. In group Pd, the incidence of these TEAEs was similar to IPd in the two older age groups, 10.7% and 5.7%, however, there was a higher incidence in the &lt;65 years age group in Pd compared to IPd (8.8% versus 1.9%).
L1447: In study EFC14335, no Grade \ge 3 TEAEs with an incidence \ge 10\% greater IPd patients aged \ge 75 years versus patients aged &lt;65 years was observed.
L1448: In study EFC14335, Grade 3-4 laboratory neutropenia occurred with similar frequency in IPd patients aged \ge 75 years (87.5%) versus &lt;65 years (88.9%), with the same trend observed also in All IPd doses (87.2% and 86.1%, respectively).
L1449: In study EFC14335, Grade 3-4 laboratory thrombocytopenia was slightly more frequent in IPd patients aged \ge 75 years (34.4%) versus &lt;65 years (29.6%), with the same trend observed also in All IPd doses (33.3% and 30.6%, respectively). A similar trend was apparent in Pd, with 28.6% in elderly patients and 22.1% in younger patients.
L1450: Among the IPd patients in the \ge 75 year age category from study EFC14335, no other notable increases in TEAEs at the SOC level were observed.
L1451: In study EFC14335, the incidence of IRs was lower in the \ge 75 age category (28.1%) compared with the &lt;65 age category (46.3%); a similar trend was also observed in All IPd doses (28.2% and 50.0%, respectively).
L1452: In study EFC14335, lower respiratory AEs occurred with a similar frequency in the \ge 75 age category compared with &lt;65 (40.6% and 38.9%), and the same trend was also observed in All IPd doses (38.5% and 44.4%).
L1453: ## Gender
L1454: Overall, in the IPd arm of study EFC14335, the incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs was similar among males and females, 85.2% and 89.1%, respectively. The incidence of grade 5 TEAEs with fatal outcome during the treatment period (including deaths in the context of progressive disease), serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading treatment discontinuation was higher in males than in females, 9.1% versus 4.7%, 67.0% versus 54.7%, and 9.1% versus 4.7%; the same trend was also observed in All IPd doses (5.1% and 10.2%, 66.1% and 53.2%, and 8.5% and 3.8, respectively).
L1455: In the Pd arm, similar trends toward higher incidence in male versus female was observed for serious TEAEs (55.9% versus 51.9%) and fatal TEAEs (10.3% versus 7.4%), but not for TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation (11.8% versus 13.6%).
L1456: In study EFC14335, the SOCs with an incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs with a \ge 10\% difference in IPd males versus females consisted of Infections (47.7% versus 35.9%, with the same trend of 42.4% and 31.6% for All IPd doses, but not in Pd, 27.9% versus 32.1%, respectively), and Blood (51.1 versus 65.6%, with the same trend of 54.2% and 69.6% also in All IPd doses, and 33.8% and 45.7% in the Pd arm). Among these SOCs, neutropenia (40.9% versus 53.1%) was the only Grade \ge 3 TEAE with a \ge 10\% difference of incidence between males versus females, with a similar trend of 44.9% and 59.5% also in All IPd doses, and 26.5% and 37.0% in the Pd arm.
L1457: ## Race
L1458: In the IPd arm of study EFC14335, the number of non-white patients was 24 and in Pd was 19, and the number of white patients in IPd was 116 and in Pd was 122. In the IPd arm, non-white and white patients were similar in the incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs (91.7% versus 87.1%), fatal TEAEs (4.2% versus 6.0%), and TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation (8.3% versus 7.8%). In groups IPd and Pd, the incidence of serious TEAEs was lower in non-white patients compared to white patients (45.8% versus 64.7% in IPd and 36.8% versus 56.6% in Pd).
L1459: ## ECOG performance score
L1460: In study EFC14335, the number of patients with ECOG scores of 2 or 3 in the IPd arm was 16 and in the Pd arm was 14, and the number of patients with a ECOG score of 0 or 1 in IPd was 136 and in Pd was 135. No patients with ECOG score of 3 were reported at the baseline. In study EFC14335, the incidence of serious TEAEs in patients who had ECOG scores of 2 (10.5% of patients) or 3 (0%) at baseline was the same in IPd and All IPd doses (100%). Overall, ECOG scores of 2 or 3 at baseline were associated with a numerically lower incidence of TEAEs leading to definitive treatment discontinuation (0 of 16 patients in IPd versus 4 of 14 [28.6%] in Pd) and numerically higher incidence of fatal outcomes during the treatment period (2 of 16 patients [12.5%] in IPd versus 1 of 14 patients [7.1%] in Pd). Overall, no safety signal is apparent for patients with ECOG score of 2.
L1461: ## Renal status (\ge 60, &lt; 60 mL/min/1.73\text{m}^2)
L1462: Overall, in the IPd arm of study EFC14335, the incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs was similar across renal status: 86% [74] of patients with \ge 60 mL/min/1.73\text{m}^2 and 90.7% [49] with &lt; 60 mL/min/1.73\text{m}^2. The incidence of Grade 5 TEAEs, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to definitive treatment discontinuation in patients with &lt; 60 mL/min/1.73\text{m}^2 was higher than in
L1463: patients with &gt;60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2, 9.3% versus 3.5%, 77.8% versus 51.2%, and 11.1% versus 5.8%; a similar trend was also observed in All IPd doses, with 11.3% and 4.4%, 76.1% and 51.3%, and 9.9% and 5.3%, respectively [5.1.10.1.1]. In group Pd, similar trends in renal status (\ge 60 [94 patients], &lt;60 [47 patients]) were observed for Grade 5 TEAEs (12.8% versus 6.4%), serious TEAEs (59.6% versus 51.1%), TEAEs leading to definitive treatment discontinuations (14.9% versus 11.7%).
L1464: In the IPd arm of study EFC14335, the SOCs with an incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs \ge 10\% greater in patients with &lt;60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 versus patients with \ge 60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 consisted of Infections (55.6% versus 38.4%), with a similar trend in All IPd doses (49.3% and 33.6%), and in the Pd arm of study EFC14335 (38.3% versus 28.7%), and Musculoskeletal (16.7% versus 3.5%), with a similar trend in All IPd doses (16.9% and 7.1%) and in the Pd arm of study EFC14335 (6.4% versus 3.2%). Among these same SOCs, the only Grade \ge 3 TEAE with an incidence \ge 10\% difference in IPd patients with &lt;60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 versus \ge 60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 was pneumonia (25.9% versus 12.8%), with a similar trend in All IPd (23.9% versus 14.2%) and in the Pd arm (23.4% versus 12.8%).
L1465: Grade 3-4 laboratory neutropenia was similar in EFC14335 IPd patients with renal status &lt;60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 versus renal status \ge 60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 (85.2% versus 84.9%), with the same trend in All IPd doses (85.9% versus 84.1%).
L1466: Grade 3-4 laboratory thrombocytopenia was more frequent in the EFC14335 IPd arm patients with renal status &lt;60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 versus renal status \ge 60 \text{ mL/min}/1.73 \text{ m}^2 (38.9% versus 25.6%), with the same trend in All IPd doses (39.4% versus 26.5%) and in the EFC14335 Pd arm patient (34.0% versus 19.1%).
L1467: No notable difference across renal status were observed in the other significant adverse events. In group IPd of study EFC14335, the incidence of all grade TEAEs in the infection SOC and in the cardiac disorders SOC was higher in patients with renal impairment (90.7% versus 77.9%) and (22.2% versus 11.6%); the same trend was observed in All IPd doses.
L1468: ## Hepatic status
L1469: In this analysis a comparison is made between patients with normal hepatic function (130 patients in IPd and 126 in Pd) and patients with impaired hepatic status (22 patients in IPd and 22 in Pd), but it should be noted that patients in this latter group contained only patients with mild hepatic impairment, except for 1 patient in Pd with moderate hepatic impairment.
L1470: Overall, in the IPd arm of study EFC14335, the incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs was greater in patients with mild hepatic impairment (95.5%) versus normal hepatic status (85.4%).
L1471: In comparison to patients with normal hepatic status, IPd patients in study EFC14335 with mild hepatic impairment had a greater incidence of Grade 5 TEAEs (3 [13.6%] patients versus 8 [6.2%]) but the incidence of TEAEs leading treatment discontinuation and of serious TEAEs was similar (2 [9.1%] versus 9 [6.9%], and 14 [63.6%] versus 80 [61.5%], respectively. Similar
L1472: trends were noted for these TEAEs, respectively, in All IPd doses (3 [12.0%] versus 13 [7.6%], 2 [8.0%] versus 11 [6.4%], and 15 [60.0%] versus 105 [61.0%]), and in EFC14335 Pd for Grade 5 TEAEs (3 [13.6%] versus 10 [7.9%]) and for serious TEAEs (11 [50.0%] versus 68 [54%]), but in the Pd arm, the incidence of the TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation was greater in patients with mild hepatic impairment (7 [31.8%]) compared to normal status (12 [9.5%]).
L1473: In study EFC14335, no SOCs with an incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs \ge 10\% greater in IPd patients with mild hepatic impairment versus patients with normal status was observed. Grade \ge 3 TEAEs with a \ge 10\% difference in incidence in patients with mild impairment versus normal status consisted of thrombocytopenia (27.3% versus 9.2%), the same trend was also observed in All IPd doses (24.0% and 9.3%), and in Pd (18.2% and 11.1%).
L1474: In study EFC14335, the incidence of Grade 3-4 laboratory neutropenia was similar in IPd patients with mild hepatic impairment (86.4%) versus normal status (84.6%), with the same trend in All IPd doses (84.0% and 84.3%), while in Pd the incidence was higher in patients with hepatic impairment (86.4% and 65.9%). Grade 3-4 laboratory thrombocytopenia was more frequent in IPd patients with mild hepatic impairment (50.0%) versus normal status (27.7%) with the same trend in All IPd doses (48.0% and 29.1%) and Pd (40.9% and 20.6%), and no notable difference across the hepatic status was observed for other significant adverse events.
L1475: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1476: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of safety analysis by age in the &lt;65, 65-75 and \ge 75 years age groups on Study EFC14335. There was a higher rate of SAEs and treatment discontinuations in the \le 75 years age group compared to the &lt;65 years age group.
L1477: However, FDA does not agree with the Applicant's statement "The IPd regimen can be administered safely to patients \ge 75 years without impact on the incidence of adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation or fatal outcomes compared to the Pd regimen." This statement is incorrect and misleading. The number of patients in the \ge 75 years age group is very small limiting definitive conclusion regarding the safety of isatuximab, pomalidomide and dexamethasone in this age subgroup of patients.
L1478: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of safety by gender and renal impairment on Study EFC14335. FDA did not conduct separate safety analyses by race, ECOG subgroups and hepatic impairment as the number of patients in individual subgroups within each of these categories in Study 14335 were very small precluding definitive conclusions regarding safety in these subgroups. Study 14335 enrolled only 1 patient with moderate or severe hepatic impairment. FDA also did not independently confirm safety in the demographic subgroups for all IPd doses.
L1479: From the FDA clinical pharmacology review "FDA agrees with the applicant's position that no therapeutic individualization is needed based on Ig type multiple myeloma (i.e., IgG vs non-IgG), demographic factors (age, sex), in patients with hepatic, or in patients with renal impairment based on population PK analyses. An effect of age, sex, renal and hepatic impairment is not
L1480: expected as isatuximab is a monoclonal antibody and its disposition is not expected to be impacted by renal or hepatic impairment. Body weight was identified as a significant covariate in the population PK analysis, with an increase in CL with increasing body weight, which supports the weight-based dosing approach."

L1483: No specific studies are planned.

L1485: [The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.]
L1486: ## 8.2.7. Additional Safety Explorations
L1487: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1488: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1489: No genotoxicity studies have been performed to evaluate genotoxicity as this drug is a mAb with no potential to interact with cellular DNA; nor have carcinogenicity studies been conducted, as this drug is a therapeutic mAb being developed for oncology indications. There is no evidence or scientific rationale indicating a potential risk for tumorigenicity.
L1490: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1491: The FDA concurs that no genotoxicity or carcinogenicity studies have been conducted. However, in the pivotal trial EFC14335, a higher rate of SPMs were reported in the isatuximab, pomalidomide, dexamethasone arm compared to the pomalidomide dexamethasone arm. Patients treated with IPd combination regimen should be monitored for the development of SPMs as per IMWG guidelines.
L1492: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1493: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1494: Pregnant or lactating females were excluded from the clinical development program. No pregnancies have been reported in any data pool.
L1495: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1496: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment above. The USPI will include the following information: "Isatuximab can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman". The assessment of isatuximab-irfc-associated risks on pregnancy is based on the mechanism of action and data from target antigen CD38 knockout animal models. There are no available data on isatuximab use in pregnant women to evaluate for a drug-associated risk of major birth defects, miscarriage or adverse maternal or fetal outcomes. Animal reproduction toxicity studies have not been conducted with isatuximab-irfc.
L1497: The combination of SARCLISA and pomalidomide is contraindicated in pregnant women because pomalidomide may cause birth defects and death of the unborn child."
L1498: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1499: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1500: No studies have been completed in the pediatric population.
L1501: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1502: The FDA agrees that no studies have been conducted in the pediatric population.
L1503: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1504: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1505: An overdose (accidental or intentional) of study treatment was defined as a \ge 30\% increase from the dose planned to be administered in the specified duration or if the dose was administered in less than half the recommended duration of administration. Reports of overdose were considered to be AESIs and were to be reported to the sponsor within 24 hours. A dexamethasone overdose was reported as an AESI in 1 patient in group Pd in study EFC14335. No patients with isatuximab overdose have been reported in any Data Pool.
L1506: Drug abuse and withdrawal and rebound have not been reported.
L1507: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1508: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Patients were treated at doses up to 20 mg/kg intravenously in clinical studies.
L1509: ## 8.2.8. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1510: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1511: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1512: At the time of this filing, isatuximab is not approved for marketing in any country.
L1513: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1514: The Agency agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1515: ## 8.2.9. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1516: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1517: The safety profile of isatuximab in combination with pomalidomide and dexamethasone has been carefully evaluated compared to pomalidomide and dexamethasone in patients with RRMM in study EFC14335, supported by a Phase 1b study with the same combination (TCD14079 Part A). Although some TEAEs were more frequent in the IPd arm, the overall safety profile is generally favorable. No dose dependency in the isatuximab safety profile was seen through the analyses of the single agent studies in Pool 2.
L1518: The duration of exposure in study EFC14335 was almost twice as long in the IPd arm compared with the Pd arm (median of 41 weeks versus 24 weeks).
L1519: In study EFC14335, TEAEs leading to definitive treatment discontinuation were not higher in IPd compared to Pd, 7.2% and 12.8% of the patients, respectively; The most frequent SOCs leading to treatment discontinuations were Infections and infestations (2.6% versus 5.4% in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively), and Blood and lymphatic system disorders (0.7% versus 4.7%).
L1520: During the treatment period, a similar number of deaths were reported in the IPd arm and Pd arms (7.2% and 8.7%, respectively), with 3 and 6 patients, respectively, having died because of AEs.
L1521: The overall incidence of serious TEAE was 61.8% and 53.7% in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively. The most common serious TEAEs in the IPd arm were pneumonia (15.1% and 15.4% of the patients in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively), and febrile neutropenia (6.6% and 2.0%). When serious TEAEs were adjusted for the longer duration of exposure in the IPd arm, however, their overall incidence rates in patient-years were similar (1.36 and 1.30 in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively).
L1522: In study EFC14335, the median duration of the isatuximab infusions was 3.3 hours during the first infusion, and 2.8 hours for the subsequent infusions, consistent with the experience across the patients treated with IPd (3.4 hours, and 2.8 hours, respectively). An alternative fixed infusion volume method was evaluated in the study TCD14079 Part B to simplify the method of administration of isatuximab and abbreviate its infusion time; based on the results from the interim analysis with 34 patients, this method allowed a considerably shorter median duration of 75 min (1.25 hours) from 3rd infusion onwards, without affecting the risk of IRs (incidence of IRs was 47.1% with no Grade \ge 3 IRs reported, with no IR beyond first infusion). The fixed-volume method also reduces the steps required for the preparation of isatuximab prior to its administration, thereby reducing the possibility of human errors and inappropriate dosing of isatuximab to patients.
L1523: Infusion reactions were reported in 38.2% of the patients in the IPd arm of study EFC14335. Most of the patients had Grade 1 (3.9%) or Grade 2 (31.6%) IRs; Grade 3 and Grade 4 IRs were reported each in 1.3% of the patients, and no fatal IRs occurred. The majority of the patients with IRs had a single episode. All the patients with IRs had their first onset during the first infusion, and on the same day of the infusion. All the IRs resolved without sequelae, most frequently on the same day. No delayed IRs occurred, and no post-infusion prophylactic
L1524: medications against the risk of IRs were administered, in accordance with the study protocol. No IRs occurred after the 4th infusion.
L1525: In most cases, the IRs were managed with infusion interruption and/or medications. Isatuximab treatment discontinuation occurred in only 4 patients (2.6%) who could continue their treatment with Pd, as per protocol.
L1526: Besides infusion reactions, the most frequent all grade treatment-emergent AEs in study EFC14335 reported with a higher frequency in the IPd arm were neutropenia (46.7% and 33.6% in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively), upper respiratory tract infection (28.3% and 17.4%), diarrhea (25.7% and 19.5%), and bronchitis (23.7% and 8.7%).
L1527: In study EFC14335, within the SOC of Infections and infestations, with the exception of urinary tract infection (4.6% and 1.3% in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively), the most frequently reported Grade \ge 3 infections occurred with a similar incidence in the IPd and Pd arms: pneumonia (16.4% and 15.4% in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively), bronchitis (3.3% and 0.7%), and lower respiratory tract infection (3.3% and 2.7%). There was no difference in the incidence of infections leading to definitive treatment discontinuation or leading to fatal outcome between the treatment arms (2.6% in IPd versus 4.0% in Pd). When adjusted for the longer duration of exposure in the IPd arm, the incidence of treatment-emergent serious infections was similar (0.72 versus 0.67 incidence rate per patient year in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively).
L1528: The incidence and severity of lower respiratory TEAEs in the IPd arm (36.8%; Grade \ge 3: 7.9%) was higher compared with the Pd arm (25.5%; Grade \ge 3: 3.4%). The most frequently reported lower respiratory TEAEs, however, occurred with similar incidences in the 2 treatment arms and included dyspnea (15.1% and 10.1% in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively), and cough (9.2% and 7.4%). No patient in the IPd arm definitively discontinued treatment because of a lower respiratory TEAE. In the IPd arm, 1 patient prematurely discontinued pomalidomide because of pneumonitis.
L1529: In study EFC14335, SPMs were reported in one (0.7%) patient in the Pd arm and in 6 (3.9%) patients in the IPd arm, and consisted of cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (cSCC) in the patient in Pd arm; in the IPd arm, cSCC occurred in 4 patients, breast post-radiation angiosarcoma in 1, and MDS in another patient. None of the patients discontinued their study treatment because of an SPM, with the exception of the patient who developed MDS with transformation to AML. All the patients with SPMs had been previously exposed to lenalidomide and alkylating agents.
L1530: Across all the isatuximab studies in Pool 4, the overall incidence of SPMs was 3.0%, with a median from the first dose of study treatment to the diagnosis of 7.16 months (range 0.5 to 26.5). The background incidence of SPMs in patients with relapsed/refractory multiple myeloma has been reported to be as high as 6%, and recent reports from clinical trials studying daratumumab in combination with bortezomib/dexamethasone and Pd in MM, the incidence of
L1531: SPMs was reported as 3.2 to 4.1% (Landgren et al 2014, Palumbo et al 2014, Areethamsirikul et al 2015, Ailawadhi et al 2018, Abildgaard et al 2018, Spencer et al 2018). Therefore, the overall incidence of SPMs observed in patients treated with isatuximab is consistent with the background incidence of SPMs in patients treated for MM, where the risk of SPMs is compounded by older age, immunosuppression due to the underlying disease, and the effect of prior exposure to alkylating agents (eg, melphalan) and to lenalidomide.
L1532: In the IPd arm, TEAEs in the SOC Nervous system disorders (40.8% versus 28.9%; Grade \ge 3: 7.9% versus 5.4%), and in the SOC Cardiac disorders (14.5% versus 4.0%; Grade \ge 3: 4.6% versus 2.0%) were more frequent than in the Pd arm. Treatment discontinuations or fatal outcomes, however, were not reported more frequently in the IPd arm because of cardiac or nervous TEAEs.
L1533: Although laboratory Grade 3-4 neutropenia was more frequent in the IPd arm (84.9% versus 69.1%), the onset and duration of these abnormalities were generally similar between treatment arms and they were reversible: in the IPd and Pd arms, the median time to the first episode of Grade 3-4 neutropenia was 22 and 21 days respectively, and the median duration of the first episode of Grade 3-4 neutropenia was 9 and 11 days, respectively. Neutropenia was handled through colony-stimulating factor use and pomalidomide dose reductions. When neutropenic infections were analyzed among those patients treated with colony stimulating factors, the rate of neutropenic infection was similar (31.4% and 32.9% in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively).
L1534: Incidence of Grade 4 thrombocytopenia and Grade 3-4 anemia were similar in both arms. The addition of isatuximab to Pd did not increase the incidence of thromboembolic AEs above what was observed for the Pd arm.
L1535: In summary, the safety profile of IPd is characterized by infusion reactions, and an increase of neutropenia and infections compared to Pd which did not interfere with the treatment duration or the overall quality of life as assessed by the Global Health Score of EORTC QLQ-C30.
L1536: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1537: FDA reviewed the applicant's position above. FDA's independent analysis of safety has been presented above in section 8.2.3. A brief summary is presented below.
L1538: FDA's integrated assessment of safety of the isatuximab, pomalidomide, dexamethasone combination focused primarily on the analysis of safety data from the pivotal Study EFC14335. Safety analyses from Study EFC14335 was based on a clinical database lock (DBL) of 22-November -2018. In study EFC14335, the safety population consisted of 301 patients with RRMM who received either IPd (152 patients) or Pd (149 patients). The duration of exposure in the pivotal study EFC14335 was almost twice as long in the IPd arm compared with the Pd arm (median 41 weeks versus 24 weeks, respectively).
L1539: Grade 3-4 TEAEs were reported more frequently in the IPd arm compared to the Pd arm (84.9% versus 69.1%) and serious TEAEs were also reported more frequently in the IPd arm compared to the Pd arm (61.8% and 53.7%). Based on the FDAs assessment using grouped terms, SAEs of pneumonia, upper respiratory tract infections and febrile neutropenia were higher in the IPd arm (\ge 5\%) compared to the Pd arm in Study 14335.
L1540: Neutropenia, infusion related reactions and upper respiratory tract infections were the most frequent adverse events leading to isatuximab dose delay, dose interruption and dose omission of isatuximab respectively.
L1541: The most frequent (\ge 20\% in the IPd arm) all grade treatment-emergent AEs in study EFC14335 reported with a higher frequency in the IPd arm were neutropenia (46.7% and 33.6% in the IPd and Pd arms, upper respiratory tract infection (56.0% and 42.0%), infusion related reactions (38.0% and none on the Pd arm), pneumonia (30.9% and 22.8%), and diarrhea (25.7% and 19.5%). Infusion related reactions, neutropenia and second primary malignancies are important safety risks noted with the isatuximab, pomalidomide and dexamethasone combination on Study 14335. The FDA considers SPMs as an important safety risk with the IPd combination based on reported higher rates in the IPd arm (3.9%) compared to the Pd arm (0.7%) in this large randomized controlled study. The USPI will include Infusion related reactions, neutropenia and second primary malignancies in the Warnings and Precautions section. Dose modifications and supportive care were effective in managing the adverse events and majority of these events resolved. The incidence of Grade 5 (fatal) TEAEs (7.9% and 9.4% in the IPd and Pd arms, respectively) and the any TEAEs leading to definitive treatment discontinuation of all study treatments (7.2% and 12.8% in the IPd and Pd arms) in study 14335 were generally low and similar between the two arms.
L1542: Routine risk minimization activities (i.e., risk communications through prescribing information, labeling, or packaging) are considered sufficient to manage the key risks associated with the use of isatuximab, pomalidomide, dexamethasone in patients with multiple myeloma who have received two prior therapies including a proteasome inhibitor and lenalidomide.
L1543: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1544: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1545: EFC14335 is a well-controlled and well-conducted randomized clinical trial. There was one planned interim analysis for overall survival and the level of significance was adjusted to account for this analysis. Since there were only 45% of the planned 220 death events for the OS analyses, it would be beneficial if the patients were followed for a longer duration and updates on the OS were provided at the later point in the future. The Applicant was asked to provide an updated OS report as a PMC. This report will be generated in approximately 2021.
L1546: Study TCD14079 was designed an early phase dose finding trial with an expansion cohort of patients treated at the recommended dose level. With 31 patients treated at the 10mg/kg dose level, the study results provided sufficient evidence of the appropriate dose selection. Due to the single-arm nature of the trial, no comparative statistical inference could be conducted.
L1547: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1548: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1549: Based on the favorable risk-benefit profile of isatuximab with pomalidomide and dexamethasone, the clinical and statistical reviewers recommend approval of: isatuximab in combination with pomalidomide and dexamethasone for the treatment of adult patients with multiple myeloma who have received at least two prior therapies including lenalidomide and proteasome inhibitor.

L1561: ## 10 Pediatrics
L1562: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1563: Under the Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA)(21 U.S.C. 355c), the requirement of pediatric studies does not apply to any drug for an indication for which orphan designation has been granted.
L1564: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1565: Isatuximab has an orphan drug designation for treatment of multiple myeloma based on Request #14-4319 and granted on May 22, 2014. Due to orphan drug designation of isatuximab for this indication, it is exempt from PREA requirements. This information was submitted in Section 1.9.1 of the BLA Application.

L1577: (b) (4) exculpatory language (i.e., mostly mild or moderate). Rounded rates per Adverse Reactions guidance. Management of infusion related reactions updated to reflect management on Study EFC14335.
(b) (4) Interference with serological testing
(b) (4) SARCLISA binds to CD38 on red blood cells (RBCs) and may result in a false positive indirect antiglobulin test (indirect Coombs test). In ICARIA-MM, the indirect antiglobulin test was positive during SARCLISA treatment in 67.7% of the tested patients. In patients with a positive indirect antiglobulin test, blood transfusions were administered without evidence of hemolysis. ABO/RhD typing was not affected by SARCLISA treatment.

Monitor patients with neutropenia for signs of infection.

This interference can impact the accuracy of the determination of complete response in some patients with IgG kappa myeloma protein.

L1596: A total of 307 patients were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to receive either SARCLISA in combination with pomalidomide and low-dose dexamethasone (Isa-Pd, 154 patients) or pomalidomide and low-dose dexamethasone (Pd, 153 patients).

L1598: Treatment was administered in both groups in 28-day cycles until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. SARCLISA 10 mg/kg was administered as an I.V. infusion weekly in the first cycle and every two weeks thereafter.
L1599: Pomalidomide 4 mg was taken orally once daily from day 1 to day 21 of each 28-day cycle. Low-dose dexamethasone (PO/IV) 40 mg (20 mg for patients \ge 75 years of age) was given on days 1, 8, 15, and 22 for each 28-day cycle.
L1600: Overall, demographic and disease characteristics at baseline were similar between the two treatment groups. The median patient age was 67 years (range 36-86), (b)(4)% of patients were \ge 75 years; 10(b)(4)% of patients entered the study with a history of COPD or asthma. L1615: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1616: The FDA's Assessment:
Clinical PMR and PMCs:
L1617: ## PMR -Clinical
L1618: Conduct long term safety monitoring of patients enrolled in study (ICARIA) titled; "A Phase 3 Randomized, Open-label, Multicenter Study Comparing Isatuximab (SAR650984) in Combination With Pomalidomide and Low-Dose Dexamethasone Versus Pomalidomide and Low-Dose Dexamethasone in Patients With Refractory or Relapsed and Refractory Multiple Myeloma", to determine the incidence of acute myeloid leukemia, myelodysplastic syndrome and other second primary malignancies in patients receiving isatuximab in combination with pomalidomide and dexamethasone and it's potential to have a detrimental impact on overall survival. Include incidence rates, time to onset, predisposing factors, and outcomes with the final report.

Trial Completion:	06/2021
Final Report Submission:	12/2021

L1622: Submit the final interim report and datasets containing at least 40 patients enrolled on studies TCD13983 VRDI Part B, TCD15484, EMN 24, EFC15992, supplemented by data from other studies with similar pre-medications, if required, to assess the frequency and severity of infusion-related reactions, occurrence of intravenous infusion interruptions, infusion rate reductions, and discontinuation during the first two infusions of isatuximab administered via the fixed-volume infusion method in patients weighing \ge 100 kg compared to patients weighing &lt;100 kg.

L1627: Refractory or Relapsed and Refractory Multiple Myeloma" to provide additional long term efficacy data that may inform product labeling.

Final Protocol Submission:	03/2020
Trial Completion:	06/2021
Final Report Submission:	12/2021
L1630: ## PMC - Clinical
L1631: Develop a validated assay to detect interference of isatuximab to any remaining endogenous M protein in the patient's serum, to facilitate determination of a complete response (CR) in patients with multiple myeloma. Complete and submit the final report of the validation study, that may provide information to prescribers about the use of the assay to determine CR.
L1632: ## PMC Schedule Milestones
L1633: 510(k) Submission:	10/2020
Final Report Submission:	02/2021
L1634: Rationale for PMR: The rate of second primary malignancies (SPM) in the pivotal trial was higher (3.9%) of patients in the IPd arm compared to (0.7%) to the Pd arm. Malignancies were primary skin cancers but also included 1 hematologic malignancy and breast cancer. Additional case of second primary malignancy prostate cancer was reported at the 120 day safety follow up in the IPd arm and none in the Pd arm. The risk for SPMs with isatuximab will be included in the label but the short follow up from the pivotal study is inadequate to characterize the risk of hematologic and solid tumor SPMs that may occur with longer exposure to isatuximab in combination with pomalidomide and dexamethasone. This PMR is to further characterize the incidence and rate of hematologic malignancies including AML, myelodysplastic syndrome and solid tumor secondary malignancies with longer follow up in isatuximab to adequately describe the serious risk of SPMs with isatuximab in the USPI. Additionally, the impact on overall survival will also be assessed.

L1708: ## 18.4.1. Pharmacometrics Review
L1709: ## 1. Applicant's Population PK analysis
L1710: ## 1.1 Introduction
L1711: The primary objectives of this analysis were to:
L1712: - Characterize the pharmacokinetics of isatuximab when administered as a single agent and in combination with dexamethasone and pomalidomide (Pd) and to obtain key exposure metrics.
- Explore factors influencing isatuximab pharmacokinetics.
L1713: ## 1.2 Model development
L1714: ## 1.2.1 Data
L1715: Data from 476 patients with multiple myeloma (MM) (7697 plasma concentrations) treated in the single agent studies (TED10893 Phase 1, Phase 2 Stage 1, Phase 2 Stage 2 and TED14154 Part A) and in combination with pomalidomide/dexamethasone studies (Phase 1b TCD14079 Part A and Phase 3 EFC14335). Isatuximab was administered as either QW, Q2W or Q2W for 8 weeks then Q4W (Q2W/Q4W) or QW for 4 weeks, then Q2W (QW/Q2W) by IV infusion at doses ranging from 1 to 20 mg/kg. Brief descriptions of the studies included are presented in Table 35.
L1716: Summary statistics of the baseline demographic covariates in the analysis dataset is provided in Table 36.
L1717: Table 35. Summary of Studies with PK Sampling Included in Population PK Analysis

L1723: (Source: Table 4, PopPK report (poh0503).
L1724: ## Base model
L1725: The final base model was described by a 2-compartment model with parallel linear and Michaelis-Menten eliminations from the central compartment (approximation of target mediated - drug disposition model) with a combined error model and inter-individual variability on all parameters. Time-varying change was modeled as a sigmoidal function (CL_{lin} = CL_{inf} * \exp [CLm * (1 - t\gamma / (KCL\gamma + t\gamma))]) with CLm being the maximum change from baseline
L1726: clearance to steady-state clearance (CL_{inf}), K_{CL} being the time estimated to achieve 50% change in linear clearance and \Gamma being the sigmoidal coefficient.
L1727: Model evaluation and selection of the base model were based on standard statistical criteria of goodness-of-fit such as a decrease in the minimum objective function value (OFV), accuracy of parameter estimation, successful model convergence, and diagnostic plots.
L1728: ## Covariate analysis
L1729: A full covariates model was developed by incorporating the effect of all pre-specified covariate parameter relationships. The final PopPK model was developed by retaining covariates that improved a goodness-of-fit statistic (MOFV). Clinical judgment, physiologic relevance, prior knowledge and mechanistic plausibility were used to determine which covariates should be tested with the various PK parameters. Covariate parameters, including age, sex, race, body surface area, body mass index, albumin, serum alkaline phosphatase, alanine amino transferase, aspartate amino transferase, bilirubin, beta-2 microglobulin, bone marrow plasma cells, serum M-protein, main Ig Type, Multiple Myeloma Type, ECOG, ISS, renal and hepatic impairment on pertinent PK parameters were evaluated.
L1730: The reviewer's analysis of covariates effect on PK was similar to that of the applicant. The effects of body weight on CL/F, V1/F, Q, and V2/F, the effects of Ig MM type and \beta2-microglobulin on CL/F are most clinically relevant and are explored in Figure 25. The model estimated covariate effects on PK parameters are illustrated in Figure 27.

L1746: Figure 27 VPC Plot for Final Covariate Model
L1747: The black lines represent the predicted median and the green lines represent the 10th, 50th and 90th percentiles of the observed concentrations. The shaded areas are the 90 % prediction intervals for the simulated datasets.
L1748: **Reviewer's comments:** The applicant's population PK analysis is acceptable for descriptive labeling purpose. The goodness-of-fit plots and the visual predictive check indicate that the population PK model is adequate in characterizing the PK profile of isatuximab in patients with RRMM. The inter-individual variability for CL_inf is moderate-high. The estimated PK parameters, such as CL_inf, V1, and V2 appear reasonable. Linear clearance was found to account for ~90% of the total clearance at 10 mg/kg QW for 4 weeks then Q2W, indicating saturation of target-mediated clearance at the recommended dosing regimen for isatuximab and Pd combination therapy. The applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. Of note, the estimated Eta shrinkage values were moderate for CL_inf and CLm (43%), and high ranging from 60% to 90% for V2, Vm, Km and Gamma. High shrinkage values associated with modeling aspects of the nonlinear and time-varying PK of isatuximab may negatively affect the confidence in the predicted values utilized in the exposure-response (E-R) analyses. The clinical relevance of covariate effects such as race and formulation on V1/F is questioned. The developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined in Table 39.
L1749: Table 39 Specific Comments on Applicant's Final Population PK model
L1750: Utility of the final model	Reviewer's Comments
L1751: Support labeling statements about intrinsic and extrinsic factors	Intrinsic factor	"Isatuximab-irfc exposure (AUC) at steady state decreases with increasing body weight. The following factors have no clinically meaningful effect on the exposure of isatuximab-irfc: age (36 to 85 years, 70 patients were >75 years old), sex, race (Caucasian, Black, Asian), renal impairment, and mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin 1 to 1.5 times upper limit of normal [ULN] or aspartate amino transferase [AST] > ULN)."
"No dose adjustments are recommended in these specific patient populations."	The statements are acceptable. The covariate analysis indicated that age, sex, race, renal function, and mild hepatic impairment cause no evident difference in PK.
Extrinsic factor	"The elimination of isatuximab-irfc was similar when given as a single agent or as combination therapy."	The statement is acceptable. The covariate analysis revealed that coadministration with Pd causes no evident difference.
Derive exposure metrics for Exposure-response analyses	Trough plasma concentration at Week 4 (CT4W), C_{max} on the first administration of Cycle 1 (C_{maxD1C1}).	High \eta shrinkage values of parameters associated with the nonlinear and time-varying PK may negatively impact the prediction of the exposure metrics used in the E-R analyses and thus limit the interpretation of E-R results.
L1752: ## 2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1753: ## 2.1 Exposure-efficacy relationship
L1754: ## 2.1.1 Applicant' analysis
L1755: Exposure-response analyses were performed for efficacy (ORR and PFS) using study EFC14335 (148 patients in the Isa-Pd arm and 149 patients in the Pd arm).
L1756: The impact of isatuximab exposure on ORR was investigated using logistic regression model with CT4W, which is defined as the C_{\text{trough}} after the 4th weekly dose. The final model included time since diagnosis and Revised-ISS (R-ISS) score and an interaction between time since diagnosis and R-ISS. The probability of an ORR was found to increase with a linear increase of CT4W, with the predicted responders below and above the median CT4W being 52% and 72%, respectively. ORR increased as time since diagnosis increased and ORR decreased with higher R-ISS stage as shown in Figure 28.
L1757: Figure 28 Model-predicted probability of ORR by R-ISS stage for 10 mg/kg QW/Q2W
L1758: Q represents the predicted proportion of responders for each quartile of CT4W group for overall population. Qsim represents the predicted proportion of responders for each quartile of CT4W group assuming all patients received 4 weekly administrations. Q 4admin represents the predicted proportion of responders for each quartile of CT4W for patients who completed 4 weekly administrations.
L1759: Source: Figure 12, Applicant's exposure-response analysis (poh0648).
L1760: While patients in all isatuximab exposure quartiles have positive treatment effect (\text{HR}&lt;1), the treatment effect is more pronounced in the 2nd quartile (\text{HR } 0.58), 3rd quartile (\text{HR } 0.48) and 4th quartile (\text{HR } 0.38) than in patients in the lower quartile of exposure. This may be due to confounded observations by the different myeloma baseline characteristics and high incidence of pomalidomide and isatuximab dose modifications in this quartile.
L1761: The impact of isatuximab exposure on PFS was investigated using Kaplan-Meier analysis where CT4W was found to be linearly associated with improved PFS. The final model included the covariates plasmacytomas, albumin and R-ISS at baseline. The PFS hazard ratio (HR) associated with CT4W quartiles are summarized in Figure 29.
L1762: Figure 29 Forest plot of HR associated with CT4W of PFS at the median, 5th and 95th percentiles by CT4W quartiles when comparing Pd arm
L1763: Source: Figure 14, Applicant's exposure-response analysis (poh0648).
L1764: ## 2.1.2 Reviewer's Comments
L1765: The reviewer's analysis was in general consistent with the applicant's results. However, the reviewer did not include the time since diagnosis as a covariate or the interaction term between time since diagnosis and R-ISS in final model of ORR. While a lower probability of ORR is expected with a higher R-ISS stage, a lower probability of ORR associated with a shorter time since diagnosis is not clinically plausible. Importantly, the reviewer notes that high eta shrinkage value associated with the PopPK modeling aspects of the nonlinear and time-varying PK of isatuximab may negatively impact the confidence levels in generation of exposure metrics used in the E-R analyses, and thus limit the interpretation of the results.
L1766: ## 2.2 Exposure-safety relationship
L1767: ## 2.2.1 Applicant's analysis
L1768: Exposure-response analyses were performed for safety (IRRs, thrombocytopenia, anemia, neutropenia, lymphopenia, infections, and respiratory events) using data pooled from studies TCD14079 Part A and EFC14335.
L1769: There was no apparent relationship between an increase of isatuximab C_{max,c1d1} and IRRs,
L1770: nor between an increase in C_{max,c1d1} or C_{post-infusion,max} and an increase in the incidence of the examined AEs of interest including, thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, lymphopenia, respiratory events, and nervous system disorders. Nevertheless, when comparing to the pomalidomide control arm (EFC14335), there was a trend of higher neutropenia, and cardiac arrhythmias and disorders in the isatuximab/pomalidomide arm, but with no difference between the quartiles of isatuximab exposure for neutropenia and a trend to less cardiac events in the highest exposure quartile.
L1771: ## **2.2.2 Reviewer's Comments**
L1772: *The reviewer did not identify an exposure-safety relationship for safety AEs. However, as summarized in Table 40, a trend towards higher incidence of respiratory infections and infestations of all grades is seen in the Isa-Pd arm compared to the Pd arm. A higher incidence of Grade 3 febrile neutropenia and Grade 4 neutropenia is seen in the Isa-Pd arm compared to the Pd arm.*

Interference & specificity	Interference with rabbit polyclonal anti-isatuximab antibodies:
1 lot of pool human K3-EDTA plasma spiked at Low (1.20 ng/mL) and High (16.0 ng/mL) levels of isatuximab. The presence of rabbit polyclonal anti-isatuximab antibodies (ADA) at dilutions equal to or lower than 10 000 may interfere with isatuximab quantitation in human K3-EDTA plasma. Range of bias: -89% to -2.0%
Interference with anti-human IgG1 antibody:
1 lot of pool human K3-EDTA plasma spiked at Low (1.20 ng/mL) and High (16.0 ng/mL) levels of isatuximab. No interference of anti-human IgG1 antibody tested at dilutions from 2000 to 200 000 was observed on isatuximab quantitation in human K3-EDTA plasma. Range of bias: -11% to +1.3%
Interference with human CD31:
1 lot of pool human K3-EDTA plasma spiked at Low (1.20 ng/mL), High (16.0 ng/mL) and SuperHigh-a (0.250 mg/mL) levels of isatuximab. No interference of human CD31, tested at 0.500 µg/mL was observed on isatuximab quantitation in human K3-EDTA plasma. Range of bias: -7.4% to +6.1%.
Interference with drugs concomitantly administered with isatuximab in clinical trials: lenalidomide, carfilzomib, dexamethasone, cyclophosphamide, pomalidomide, bortezomib and cemiplimab (RGN2810):
1 lot of pool human K3-EDTA plasma spiked at Low (1.20 ng/mL), High (16.0 ng/mL), SuperHigh-b (0.500 ng/mL) and SuperHigh-c (2.00 mg/mL) levels of isatuximab and tested separately for each drug. No impact on the isatuximab quantitation in human K3-EDTA plasma was observed whatever

At SuperHigh-b (0.500 mg/mL) levels of isatuximab, after 6 additional freeze/thaw cycles at -80°C, the bias ranged between 6.8% and 11%.

Long-term storage	The long term stability of isatuximab was conducted using the following samples:
- Spiked human K3-EDTA plasma samples at Low (1.20 ng/mL), High (16.0 ng/mL) and SuperHigh (0.250 mg/mL, 0.500 mg/mL then 2.00 mg/mL) levels of isatuximab. They were then stored at -20°C and -80°C for up to 24 months for Low, High and SuperHigh-a levels and for up to 26 months for SuperHigh-c levels at 2.00 mg/mL. The superHigh-b level was tested for up to 7 months at -20°C and for up to 13 months at -80°C
- Pools of incurred K3-EDTA plasma specimens prepared at Low (104 ng/mL), High (93774 ng/mL) and SuperHigh (1.00 mg/mL) concentration levels and collected from patients with selected CD38+ hematological malignancies and administered with isatuximab. They were then stored at -20°C or at -80°C for up to 33 months for Low and High levels and for up to 40 months for SuperHigh level.
Human K3-EDTA plasma samples spiked with isatuximab up to 2.00 mg/mL could be stored for up to 24 months at about -20°C and -80°C in polypropylene tubes.
Incurred human plasma specimens containing up to about 1.00 mg/mL of isatuximab could be stored for up to 15 months at	[Table 5], [Table 6], [Table 7], [Table 8], [Table 9] and [Table 10] of report SPH0304 (module 5.3.1.4)

L1820: Figure 4 displays two bar charts showing the concentration of IFN-\gamma (left) and TNF-\alpha (right) released from primary NK cells in response to various treatments. The Y-axis for both charts is pg/mL.
IFN-\gamma Release: The concentration is highest for Isatuximab (Isa) treatment (~500 pg/mL). Other treatments (NT, IgG1, Isa*, F(ab')_2, IgG1 + F(ab')_2) show minimal release (near 0 pg/mL).
TNF-\alpha Release: The concentration is highest for Isatuximab (Isa) treatment (~16 pg/mL). Other treatments (NT, IgG1, Isa*, F(ab')_2, IgG1 + F(ab')_2) show low release (around 4-5 pg/mL).
The treatments compared are: NT (Negative Control), IgG1, Isa (Isatuximab), Isa* (Isatuximab variant unable to bind CD38), F(ab')_2, and IgG1 + F(ab')_2.
L1821: Figure 5 displays two bar charts illustrating the percentage lysis of K562 target cells by NK cells in the presence of Isatuximab or IgG control.
The top panel shows results using NK-92 cells. The Y-axis represents % Lysis (0 to 100). The X-axis shows treatment groups: IgG and Isatuximab, each at two concentrations (0.03 \mug/ml and 0.3 \mug/ml). Lysis is significantly higher with Isatuximab (approx. 75% at 0.03 \mug/ml and 95% at 0.3 \mug/ml) compared to IgG (approx. 55% at 0.03 \mug/ml and 50% at 0.3 \mug/ml).
The bottom panel shows results using primary NK cells isolated from 7 healthy donors (Donor 1 to Donor 7). The Y-axis represents % Lysis (0 to 100). The X-axis shows Donor 1 through Donor 7. For each donor, two treatments are compared: IgG 1 \mug/ml (white bars) and Isatuximab 1 \mug/ml (black bars). Lysis is generally higher with Isatuximab across most donors, although the magnitude of increase varies. For example, Donor 2 shows high lysis (approx. 85% Isatuximab vs 80% IgG), while Donor 4 shows lower lysis (approx. 38% Isatuximab vs 35% IgG).
L1822: Figure 6 displays two bar charts showing the effect of Isatuximab (Isa) on M1 and M2 polarization of human monocytes. The Y-axis represents the percentage of CD14+CD80+ cells. The X-axis shows different treatment conditions: Medium, IFN-\gamma 50ng/ml, IL-4 20ng/ml, Isa 10\mug/ml, Isa* 10\mug/ml, and F(ab')_2 10\mug/ml. The legend indicates Monocytes (white bars) and Monocytes + NK (hatched bars).
M1 Polarization (Left Chart): IFN-\gamma 50ng/ml treatment significantly increases M1 polarization (approx. 55% of CD14+CD80+ cells) compared to Medium (approx. 10%). Isa 10\mug/ml treatment shows a moderate increase (approx. 25%). Isa* 10\mug/ml and F(ab')_2 10\mug/ml treatments show minimal or no increase in M1 polarization compared to Medium.
M2 Polarization (Right Chart): IL-4 20ng/ml treatment significantly increases M2 polarization (approx. 75% of CD14+CD80+ cells) compared to Medium (approx. 20%). Isa 10\mug/ml treatment shows a moderate increase (approx. 20%). Isa* 10\mug/ml and F(ab')_2 10\mug/ml treatments show minimal or no increase in M2 polarization compared to Medium.
L1823: Figure 7 displays two scatter plots comparing CD38 expression on T cells. The left panel shows the percentage of CD38high Subset (Y-axis, 0 to 6) for Treg and Tcon cells from healthy donors. The right panel shows the percentage of CD38high Tregs (Y-axis, 0 to 15) for MM patients and Normal donors.
Left Panel (Healthy Donors): Tregs show a significantly higher percentage of CD38high subset (approx. 3.5%) compared to Tcons (approx. 0.5%). The difference is marked with ****.
Right Panel (MM vs Normal): MM patients show a significantly higher percentage of CD38high Tregs (approx. 9%) compared to Normal donors (approx. 5%). The difference is marked with ****.
L1824: Bar chart showing Fold change (3H uptake) versus Isatuximab concentration (Isa in µg/mL) for Treg inhibition of conventional T cell proliferation. The x-axis shows concentrations 0, 0.001, 0.01, 0.1, 1, and 10 µg/mL. The y-axis shows Fold change (3H uptake) from 0 to 1,200. The chart compares Tcons only, Tregs only, and Tregs+Tcons groups. Tregs only shows minimal proliferation (around 50-100 fold change). Tcons only shows high proliferation (around 650 fold change). Tregs+Tcons shows intermediate proliferation (around 300-400 fold change). Isatuximab treatment (0.001 to 10 µg/mL) significantly increases proliferation in the Tregs+Tcons group compared to the 0 µg/mL control, with the highest fold change (around 850) observed at 10 µg/mL. Statistical significance is indicated by asterisks (* p<0.05, *** p<0.001).
L1825: Bar chart showing cADPR (Average AUC) versus Concentration (µg/mL) for IgG1, Isatuximab, and Daratumumab. The Y-axis ranges from 0.00 to 40,000.00. The X-axis shows concentrations: 0.0000, 0.0016, 0.0080, 0.0400, 0.2000, and 1.0000 µg/mL. IgG1 (white bars) consistently shows the highest cADPR AUC, indicating minimal inhibition. Isatuximab (dark gray bars) shows concentration-dependent inhibition, with AUC decreasing significantly as concentration increases. Daratumumab (light gray bars) shows intermediate inhibition, also concentration-dependent.

L1829: Figure 12 displays three scatter plots comparing Isatuximab Infusion Rate (mg/hr) for the first infusion versus Time (hr) for representative patient weights of 40 kg, 70 kg, and 120 kg. The Y-axis represents Infusion rate (mg/hr) for the first infusion, and the X-axis represents Time (hr). Data points are categorized by infusion method: weight based (circles, 'o') and fixed volume (plus signs, '+').
Patient weight 40 kg: The infusion rate increases over time for both methods. The fixed volume method generally maintains a higher infusion rate than the weight-based method.

L1843: Figure 26 displays six Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the Final Covariate Model, arranged in a 2x3 grid. The plots compare observed data (blue dots) against model predictions or residuals, using an Identity line (black) and a Spline (red).
Top Left: Population predictions
X-axis: Population predictions (0 to 1200). Y-axis: Observations (0 to 2000). The Spline closely follows the Identity line, indicating a good fit for population predictions.
Top Right: Individual predictions
X-axis: Individual predictions (0 to 1600). Y-axis: Observations (0 to 2000). The Spline closely follows the Identity line, indicating a good fit for individual predictions.
Middle Left: PWRES vs Time (weeks)
X-axis: Time (weeks) (0 to 120). Y-axis: PWRES (0 to 15). The Spline is centered around the Y=0 line, showing no systematic trend, indicating good fit.
Middle Right: PWRES vs Concentration (mg/L)
X-axis: Concentration (mg/L) (0 to 1200). Y-axis: PWRES (0 to 15). The Spline is centered around the Y=0 line, showing no systematic trend, indicating good fit.
Bottom Left: IWRES vs Time (weeks)
X-axis: Time (weeks) (0 to 120). Y-axis: IWRES (-2.5 to 5.0). The Spline is centered around the Y=0 line, showing no systematic trend, indicating good fit.
Bottom Right: IWRES vs Concentration (mg/L)
X-axis: Concentration (mg/L) (0 to 1500). Y-axis: IWRES (-2.5 to 5.0). The Spline is centered around the Y=0 line, showing no systematic trend, indicating good fit.
L1844: Visual Predictive Check (VPC) Plot for the Final Covariate Model. The plot shows Plasma Concentration (mg/L) on the Y-axis versus Time (h) on the X-axis. Observed data points are shown as black dots. The predicted median is a solid black line. The 90% prediction interval is a shaded blue area. Empirical percentiles (10th, 50th, and 90th) are shown as green lines. The plot indicates that the model adequately characterizes the PK profile, with observed data points generally falling within the prediction interval.
L1845: Figure 28: Model-predicted probability of ORR by R-ISS stage for 10 mg/kg QW/Q2W. The graph plots Predicted Prob of Responder (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) against Ctrough 4W (ug/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 400). Vertical lines mark quartiles (Q1, Q2, Q3) and corresponding simulated values (Q1.sim, Q2.sim, Q3.sim) and 4-administration values (Q1.4adm, Q2.4adm, Q3.4adm). Four curves are shown: Predicted Overall (Red solid line), Predicted for R-ISS=Stage I (Blue dashed line), Predicted for R-ISS=Stage II (Gray dashed line), and Predicted for R-ISS=Stage III (Black dashed line). The predicted probability of ORR increases with Ctrough 4W and decreases with higher R-ISS stage.
L1846: Forest plot showing the Hazard Ratio (HR) and 95% Confidence Interval (CI) for PFS associated with CT4W quartile subgroups when comparing the Pd arm (I-Pd) versus the Pd arm (Pd). The plot is titled "I-Pd CT4W quartile subgroups from all patients versus Pd".
The x-axis represents HR and [95%CI], ranging from 0.3 to 1.0. A vertical dashed line marks HR=1.0. An arrow indicates the direction of hazard, labeled "Favors I-Pd" (left of 1.0) and "Favors Pd" (right of 1.0).
